**Beijing**,[^cite_note-10] [previously romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_postal_romanization) as **Peking**,[^cite_note-13] is the [capital city](181337.md) of [China](5405.md). With more than 21.8 million residents,[^cite_note-\:1-14] it is the world's [most populous national capital city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_capitals_by_population), as well as China's [second-largest city by urban area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_China_by_population), after [Shanghai](27643.md).[^cite_note-15] It is located in [Northern China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_China) and is governed as a provincial-level [direct-administered municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct-administered_municipality) with [16 municipal districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_administrative_divisions_of_Beijing).[^cite_note-figures-16] Beijing is mostly surrounded by [Hebei Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebei_Province) and neighbors [Tianjin Municipality](50821.md) to the southeast; together, the three divisions form the [Jing-Jin-Ji cluster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jing-Jin-Ji).[^cite_note-basic-17]

Beijing is a [global city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city) and one of the world's leading centers for [culture](#Culture), [diplomacy](#International_relations), [politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Beijing), [finance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Financial_Centres_Index), [business and economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Beijing), [education](#Education_and_research), [research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_scientific_output), [language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_dialect), [tourism](#Places_of_interest), [media](#Media), [sports](#Sports), [science and technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_the_People's_Republic_of_China), [transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_China), and [art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art). It is home to the headquarters of most of [China's largest state-owned companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_largest_companies_of_China) and houses the largest number of [*Fortune* Global 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_Global_500) companies in the world, as well as the world's four biggest [financial institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_banks) by total assets.[^cite_note-18] It is also a [major hub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Beijing) for the [national highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_National_Highways_of_Beijing), [expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressways_of_Beijing), [railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_China), and [high-speed rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-speed_rail_in_China) networks. For a decade before the [COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_China) pandemic, [Beijing Capital International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Capital_International_Airport) was [Asia's busiest airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_busiest_airports_in_Asia) (2009–2019) and the [second-busiest airport in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World's_busiest_airports_by_passenger_traffic) (2010–2019).[^cite_note-ACI_aero-19] In 2020, the [Beijing subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Subway) was the fourth-busiest and second-longest [in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_metro_systems).[^cite_note-20] [Beijing Daxing International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Daxing_International_Airport), Beijing's second international airport, is the largest single-structure [airport terminal](423849.md) in the world.[^cite_note-21][^cite_note-22] The city has hosted numerous international and national sporting events, the most notable being the [2008 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics) and [2008 Summer Paralympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Paralympics) Games. In 2022, Beijing became the first city to host both the [Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics) and [Winter Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Winter_Olympics),[^cite_note-BZ-23] and also the [Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Paralympics) and [Winter Paralympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Winter_Paralympics).[^cite_note-IA-24]

The architecture of Beijing contains elements of both traditional Chinese architecture and modern styles, with one half of the city being modernized and renovated, and the other half still offering traditional *hutong* districts.[^cite_note-25] Beijing is [one of the oldest cities in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_continuously_inhabited_cities), with recorded [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Beijing) spanning over three millennia. As the last remaining one of the [Four Great Ancient Capitals of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_capitals_of_China), Beijing has been the political center of the country for most of the past eight centuries[^cite_note-26] and was the [largest city in the world by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_cities_throughout_history) for much of the [second millennium AD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2nd_millennium).[^cite_note-Top_Ten_Cities_By_Population_Throughout_History-27] With mountains surrounding the inland city on three sides, in addition to the [ancient city fortifications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_city_fortifications), Beijing was strategically poised to be and developed into the residence of the [emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emperor_of_China). The city is renowned for its opulent palaces, temples, parks, gardens, tombs, [walls and gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_city_fortifications).[^cite_note-world_book-28] Beijing is considered one of the most well-known tourist destinations in the world. In 2018, Beijing was the second highest earning [tourist city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourist_city) in the world after [Shanghai](27643.md).[^cite_note-29] Beijing is home to many national [monuments and museums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_China#Beijing) and has [eight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_China) UNESCO [World Heritage Sites](44940.md)—the [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City), [Temple of Heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Heaven), [Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Palace), [Ming Tombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_Tombs), [Zhoukoudian Peking Man Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhoukoudian_Peking_Man_Site), [Beijing Central Axis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Central_Axis) and parts of the [Great Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall) and the [Grand Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Canal_\(China\))—all of which are popular tourist locations.[^cite_note-30] [Siheyuans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siheyuan), the city's traditional housing style, and [hutongs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutong), the narrow alleys between siheyuans, are major tourist attractions and are common in urban Beijing.

[Beijing's public universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_and_colleges_in_Beijing) make up more than one-fifth of [Double First-Class Construction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_First-Class_Construction) universities, and many of them consistently [rank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rankings_of_universities_in_China) among the best in the Asia-Pacific and the world,[^cite_note-BG-31][^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33] including [Tsinghua University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsinghua_University), [Peking University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_University) and [UCAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_Chinese_Academy_of_Sciences).[^cite_note-34][^cite_note-EE-35][^cite_note-36] [Beijing CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_CBD) is a center for [Beijing's economic expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Beijing), with the ongoing or recently completed construction of [multiple skyscrapers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Beijing). Beijing's [Zhongguancun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongguancun) area is a world leading center of scientific and technological innovation, as well as entrepreneurship. Beijing has been ranked the city with the largest scientific research output by the [Nature Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_Index) since the list's inception in 2016.[^cite_note-SC-37][^cite_note-BD-38] Beijing hosts [176 foreign embassies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_China), as well as the headquarters of many organizations, including the [Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Infrastructure_Investment_Bank), the [Shanghai Cooperation Organisation](152422.md), the [Silk Road Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road_Fund), the [Chinese Academy of Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Sciences), the [Chinese Academy of Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Engineering), the [Chinese Academy of Social Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Social_Sciences), the [Central Academy of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Academy_of_Fine_Arts), the [Central Academy of Drama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Academy_of_Drama), the [Central Conservatory of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Conservatory_of_Music), and the [Red Cross Society of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Cross_Society_of_China).

## Etymology

Main article: [Names of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_Beijing)

Over the past 3,000 years, the city of Beijing has had [numerous other names](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_Beijing). The name *Beijing*, which means 'Northern Capital' (from the [Chinese characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_characters) 北 *běi* for 'north' and 京 *jīng* for 'capital'), was applied to the city in 1403 during the [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty) to distinguish the city from [Nanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing) (南京, 'Southern Capital').[^cite_note-minggov-39] The English spelling *Beijing* is based on the government's official [romanization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Chinese) (adopted in the 1980s) of the two characters as they are pronounced in [Standard Mandarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Mandarin). An older English spelling, *Peking*, was used by Jesuit missionary [Martino Martini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martino_Martini) in a popular atlas published in Amsterdam in 1655.[^cite_note-40] Although Peking is no longer the common name for the city,[^cite_note-41] some of the city's older locations and facilities, such as [Beijing Capital International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Capital_International_Airport), with the [IATA code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Air_Transport_Association_code) PEK, and [Peking University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_University),[^cite_note-42][^cite_note-43] still retain the former romanization.

The single Chinese character abbreviation for Beijing is [京](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E4%BA%AC), which appears on automobile license plates in the city. The official Latin alphabet [abbreviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standardization_Administration_of_China) for Beijing is "BJ".[^cite_note-44]

## History

Main article: [History of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Beijing)

### Early history

The earliest traces of human habitation in the Beijing municipality were found in the caves of [Dragon Bone Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragon_Bone_Hill) near the village of [Zhoukoudian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhoukoudian) in [Fangshan District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fangshan_District), where [Peking Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_Man) lived. *[Homo erectus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homo_erectus)* fossils from the caves date to 230,000 to 250,000 years ago. [Paleolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleolithic) *[Homo sapiens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homo_sapiens)* also lived there more recently, about 27,000 years ago.[^cite_note-45] Archaeologists have found [neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) settlements throughout the municipality, including in [Wangfujing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wangfujing), located in central Beijing.

The first [walled city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Chinese_urban_planning) in Beijing was [Ji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jicheng_\(Beijing\)), the eponymous capital city of the [state of Ji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Ji). Within modern Beijing, Ji was located around the present [Guang'anmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guang'anmen) area in the south of [Xicheng District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xicheng_District).[^cite_note-Haw1-46][^cite_note-autogenerated1-47] During the early [Zhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhou_dynasty) period, this settlement was conquered by the [state of Yan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Yan) (11th century BC – 222 BC), whose original capital was located 45 kilometres (28 mi) southwest in Fangshan District, and made its new capital. Yan eventually became one of the seven major states of China during the [Warring States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warring_States) period (c. 475 – 221 BC).[^cite_note-Haw1-46]

### Early and Mid-Imperial China

After the [First Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_Shi_Huang) [unified China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin's_wars_of_unification) in 221 BC, Ji became the capital of [Guangyang Commandery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangyang_Commandery). During the [Han dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_dynasty) (202 BC – 220 AD), the city was first the capital of the subordinate [Kingdom of Yan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yan_Kingdom_\(Han_dynasty\)), and later again the seat of Guangyang Commandery after the kingdom was abolished. The Han divided the empire into 13 provinces, with Ji being the capital of [You Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Province).[^cite_note-48] During the [Three Kingdoms period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Kingdoms_period), it was held by [Gongsun Zan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gongsun_Zan) and [Yuan Shao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_Shao) before falling to [Cao Cao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cao_Cao). The AD third-century [Western Jin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin_dynasty_\(265%E2%80%93420\)) demoted the town, placing the prefectural seat in neighboring [Zhuozhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhuo_Prefecture). During the [Sixteen Kingdoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixteen_Kingdoms) period when northern China was conquered and divided by the [Wu Hu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Barbarians), Ji was briefly the capital of the [Xianbei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xianbei) [Former Yan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Former_Yan) Kingdom.[^cite_note-Rene-49]



The Mahāvīra Hall of [Tanzhe Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanzhe_Temple), founded in 307 CE during the [Western Jin dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin_dynasty_\(266%E2%80%93420\))

After China was reunified by the [Sui dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sui_dynasty) in 581, Ji, as the seat of [Zhuo Commandery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhuo_Commandery), became the northern terminus of the [Grand Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Canal_\(China\)). Under the [Tang dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tang_dynasty), Ji as the center of [You Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Prefecture) (Youzhou), served as a military frontier command center. During the [An-Shi Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An-Shi_Rebellion) and again amidst the turmoil of the mid-Tang period, the rebel commander [An Lushan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An_Lushan) founded the short-lived Yan dynasty and called the city [Yanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yanjing), or the "Yan Capital". Also in the Tang dynasty, the city's name Ji was replaced by Youzhou or Yanjing. In 938, after the fall of the [Later Tang dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Later_Tang), the [Later Jin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Later_Jin_\(Five_Dynasties\)) ceded [the frontier territory including what is now Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixteen_Prefectures) to the [Khitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khitan_people) Liao dynasty, which treated the city as [Nanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liao_Nanjing), or the "Southern Capital", one of four secondary capitals to complement its "Supreme Capital" [Shangjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shangjing_Linhuangfu) (in modern [Baarin Left Banner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baarin_Left_Banner), [Inner Mongolia](55064.md)). Some of the [oldest surviving pagodas in Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pagodas_in_Beijing) date to the Liao period, including the [Tianning Pagoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pagoda_of_Tianning_Temple_\(Beijing\)).



The [Tianning Pagoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pagoda_of_Tianning_Temple_\(Beijing\)), built around 1120 during the [Liao dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liao_dynasty), one of the oldest surviving structures in Beijing

The Liao fell to the [Jurchen Jin dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin_dynasty_\(1115%E2%80%931234\)) in 1122, which gave the city to the [Song dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_dynasty) and then retook it in 1125 during its [conquest of northern China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin%E2%80%93Song_Wars). In 1153, the Jurchen Jin made Beijing their "Central Capital", or [Zhongdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongdu).[^cite_note-hist-50] The city was besieged by [Genghis Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genghis_Khan)'s invading [Mongolian army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_Empire) in 1213 and [razed to the ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Zhongdu) two years later.[^cite_note-economist-51] Two generations later, [Kublai Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kublai_Khan) ordered the construction of [Dadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khanbaliq) (or Daidu to the Mongols, commonly known as [Khanbaliq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khanbaliq)), a new capital for his [Yuan dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_dynasty) to the northeast of the Zhongdu ruins. The construction took from 1264 to 1293,[^cite_note-hist-50][^cite_note-economist-51][^cite_note-52] but greatly enhanced the status of a city on the northern fringe of [China proper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_proper). The city was centered on the [Drum Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_Tower_and_Bell_Tower_of_Beijing) slightly to the north of modern Beijing and stretched from the present-day [Chang'an Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang'an_Avenue) to the northern part of [Line 10 subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_10,_Beijing_Subway). Remnants of the Yuan [rammed earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rammed_earth) wall still stand and are known as the Tucheng.[^cite_note-53]

### Ming dynasty



A [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty) silk hanging scroll depicting the [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City) with its architect [Kuai Xiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuai_Xiang) standing before Chengtian Gate (present-day Tiananmen); [British Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Museum), London

In 1368, soon after declaring the new [Hongwu era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongwu_Emperor) of the [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty), the [rebel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Turban_rebellion) leader [Zhu Yuanzhang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhu_Yuanzhang) captured Dadu/Khanbaliq and razed the Yuan palaces to the ground.[^cite_note-patbu-54] Since the [Yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Yuan_dynasty) continued to occupy [Shangdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xanadu,_China) and [Mongolia](19271.md), Dadu was used to supply the Ming military garrisons in the area and renamed Beiping ([Wade–Giles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wade%E2%80%93Giles): Peip'ing, "Northern Peace").[^cite_note-55] Under the Hongwu Emperor's feudal policies, Beiping was given to his son [Zhu Di](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhu_Di), who was created "[Prince of Yan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Yan)".

The early death of [Zhu Yuanzhang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhu_Yuanzhang)'s heir led to a [succession struggle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jingnan_Campaign) upon his death, one that ended with the victory of [Zhu Di](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhu_Di) and the declaration of the new [Yongle era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yongle_Emperor). Since his harsh treatment of the Ming capital [Yingtian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing) (modern [Nanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing)) alienated many there, he established his fief as a new co-capital. The city of Beiping became Beijing ("Northern Capital") or [Shuntian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shuntian_Prefecture)[^cite_note-WDL1-56] in 1403.[^cite_note-minggov-39] The construction of the new imperial residence, the [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City), took from 1406 to 1420;[^cite_note-economist-51] this period was also responsible for several other of the modern city's major attractions, such as the [Temple of Heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Heaven)[^cite_note-57] and [Tian'anmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tian'anmen). On 28 October 1420, the city was officially designated the capital of the [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty) in the same year that the Forbidden City was completed.[^cite_note-Hymes-58] Beijing became the empire's primary capital, and Yingtian, also called [Nanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing) ("Southern Capital"), became the co-capital. (A 1425 order by Zhu Di's son, the [Hongxi Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongxi_Emperor), to return the primary capital to Nanjing was never carried out: he died, probably of a heart attack, the next month. He was buried, like almost every Ming emperor to follow him, in an [elaborate necropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_tombs) to Beijing's north.)

By the 15th century, Beijing had essentially taken its current shape. The [Ming city wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_city_fortifications) continued to serve until 1965, when it was pulled down and the [Beijing Subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Subway) was built on its foundations.[^cite_note-59] It is generally believed that Beijing was the largest city in the world for most of the 15th, 16th, 17th, and 18th centuries.[^cite_note-60] The [first known church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_China) was constructed by [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuit_China_Missions) in 1652 at the former site of [Matteo Ricci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Ricci)'s chapel; the modern [Nantang Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_the_Immaculate_Conception_in_Beijing) was later built upon the same site.[^cite_note-61]

The capture of Beijing by [Li Zicheng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Zicheng)'s peasant army in 1644 ended the dynasty, but he and his [Shun court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shun_dynasty) abandoned the city without a fight when the [Manchu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchu_people) army of Prince [Dorgon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorgon) of the [Qing dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty) arrived 40 days later.

### Qing dynasty

Beijing was declared the sole capital of the empire in 1644.[^cite_note-britannica_ming_qing-62][^cite_note-63] The Qing emperors made some modifications to the Imperial residence but, in large part, the Ming buildings and the general layout remained unchanged. Facilities for Manchu worship were introduced, but the Qing also continued the traditional state rituals. Signage was bilingual or Chinese. This early Qing Beijing later formed the setting for the Chinese novel *[Dream of the Red Chamber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dream_of_the_Red_Chamber)*. Northwest of the city, Qing emperors built several large palatial gardens including the [Old Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Summer_Palace) and the [Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Palace).

During the [Second Opium War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Opium_War), Anglo-French forces captured the outskirts of the city, looting and burning the [Old Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Summer_Palace) in 1860. Under the [Convention of Peking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_of_Peking)[^cite_note-66] ending that war, Western powers for the first time secured the right to establish [permanent diplomatic presences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Legation_Quarter) within the city. From 14 to 15 August 1900 the [Battle of Peking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Peking_\(1900\)) was fought. This battle was part of the [Boxer Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxer_Rebellion). The attempt by the [Boxers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxers_\(group\)) to eradicate this presence, as well as [Chinese Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_christian) converts, led to Beijing's reoccupation by eight [foreign powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eight-Nation_Alliance).[^cite_note-67] During the fighting, several important structures were destroyed, including the [Hanlin Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanlin_Academy) and the (new) [Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Palace). A [peace agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_agreement) was concluded between the [Eight-Nation Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eight-Nation_Alliance) and representatives of the Chinese government [Li Hongzhang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Hongzhang) and [Yikuang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yikuang) on 7 September 1901. The treaty required China to pay an indemnity of US\$335 million (over US\$4 billion in current dollars) plus interest over a period of 39 years. Also required was the execution or exile of government supporters of the Boxers and the destruction of Chinese forts and other defenses in much of northern China. Ten days after the treaty was signed the foreign armies left Beijing, although legation guards would remain there until [World War II](32927.md).[^cite_note-68]

With the treaty signed the [Empress Dowager Cixi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empress_Dowager_Cixi) returned to Beijing from her "tour of inspection" on 7 January 1902 and the rule of the Qing dynasty over China was restored, albeit much weakened by the defeat it had suffered in the Boxer Rebellion and by the indemnity and stipulations of the peace treaty.[^cite_note-69] The Dowager died in 1908 and the dynasty imploded in 1911.

### Republic of China

The fomenters of the [Xinhai Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinhai_Revolution) of 1911 sought to replace Qing rule with a republic and leaders like [Sun Yat-sen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Yat-sen) originally intended to return the capital to [Nanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing). After the Qing general [Yuan Shikai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_Shikai) forced the abdication of the last Qing emperor and ensured the success of the revolution, the revolutionaries accepted him as president of the new [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\)). Yuan maintained his capital at Beijing and quickly consolidated power, declaring himself emperor in 1915. His death less than a year later[^cite_note-70] left China under the control of the warlords commanding the regional armies. Following the success of the [Kuomintang](16903.md)'s [Northern Expedition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Expedition_\(1926%E2%80%931927\)), the capital was formally moved to [Nanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing) in 1928. On 28 June the same year, Beijing's name was returned to Beiping (written at the time as "Peiping").[^cite_note-columbia_encyclopaedia-71][^cite_note-72]

On 7 July 1937, the 29th Army and the Japanese army in China exchanged fire at the [Marco Polo Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Polo_Bridge) near the [Wanping Fortress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wanping_Fortress) southwest of the city. The [Marco Polo Bridge Incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Polo_Bridge_Incident) triggered the [Second Sino-Japanese War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Sino-Japanese_War), [World War II](32927.md) as it is known in China. During the war,[^cite_note-columbia_encyclopaedia-71] Beijing fell to Japan on 29 July 1937[^cite_note-73] and was made the seat of the [Provisional Government of the Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Government_of_the_Republic_of_China_\(1937%E2%80%931940\)), a [puppet state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puppet_state) that ruled the ethnic-Chinese portions of Japanese-occupied [northern China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_China).[^cite_note-74] This government was later merged into the larger [Wang Jingwei government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reorganized_National_Government_of_China) based in Nanjing.[^cite_note-75]

### People's Republic of China

In the final phases of the [Chinese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Civil_War), the [People's Liberation Army](66890.md) seized control of the city peacefully on 31 January 1949 in the course of the [Pingjin Campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pingjin_Campaign). On 1 October that year, [Mao Zedong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mao_Zedong) announced the creation of the [People's Republic of China](5405.md) from atop [Tiananmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiananmen). He restored the name of the city, as the new capital, to Beijing,[^cite_note-76] a decision that had been reached by the [Chinese People's Political Consultative Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_People's_Political_Consultative_Conference) just a few days earlier.

In the 1950s, the city began to expand beyond the old walled city and its surrounding neighborhoods, with heavy industries in [the west](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shijingshan_District) and residential neighborhoods in [the north](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hepingli_Subdistrict,_Beijing). Many areas of the [Beijing city wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_city_wall) were torn down in the 1960s to make way for the construction of the [Beijing Subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Subway) and the [2nd Ring Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2nd_Ring_Road).

During the [Cultural Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Revolution) from 1966 to 1976, the [Red Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Guard_\(China\)) movement began in Beijing and the city's government fell victim to one of the first purges. By the autumn of 1966, all city schools were shut down and over a million Red Guards from across the country gathered in Beijing for eight rallies in Tiananmen Square with Mao.[^cite_note-77] The campaign took a devastating toll on Beijing's built heritage: by the end of the Cultural Revolution, 4,922 of the 6,843 sites officially designated as historically significant in the city had been destroyed or damaged, over 70 percent of the total, as Red Guards systematically targeted temples, shrines, ancestral halls, and monuments in the campaign to eliminate the "[Four Olds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Olds)".[^cite_note-78]

In April 1976, a large public gathering of Beijing residents in Tiananmen Square, protesting against the [Gang of Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gang_of_Four) and the Cultural Revolution, [was forcefully suppressed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Tiananmen_incident). In October 1976, the Gang was arrested in [Zhongnanhai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongnanhai) and the Cultural Revolution came to an end. In December 1978, the [Third Plenum of the 11th Party Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Plenary_Session_of_the_11th_CPC_Central_Committee) in Beijing under the leadership of [Deng Xiaoping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deng_Xiaoping) reversed the verdicts against victims of the Cultural Revolution and instituted the [reform and opening up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_and_opening_up).

Since the early 1980s, the urban area of Beijing has expanded greatly with the completion of the 2nd Ring Road in 1981 and the subsequent addition of the [3rd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3rd_Ring_Road_\(Beijing\)), [4th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_Ring_Road), [5th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5th_Ring_Road) and [6th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6th_Ring_Road) ring roads.[^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80] According to one 2005 newspaper report, the newly developed Beijing was one-and-a-half times its previous size.[^cite_note-81] [Wangfujing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wangfujing) and [Xidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xidan) have developed into flourishing shopping districts,[^cite_note-82] while [Zhongguancun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongguancun) has become a major center of electronics in China.[^cite_note-83] In recent years, the expansion of Beijing has also brought to the forefront some problems of urbanization, such as [heavy traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_congestion), [poor air quality](10934212.md), the loss of historic neighborhoods, and a significant influx of migrant workers from less-developed [rural areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rural_area) of the country.[^cite_note-84] The city has also been the location of many significant events in recent Chinese history, principally the [Tiananmen Square protests of 1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiananmen_Square_protests_of_1989).[^cite_note-BBC_News-85] Beijing has hosted major international sporting events, including the [2008 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics), the [2015 World Athletics Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_World_Athletics_Championships), and the [2022 Winter Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Winter_Olympics), and is the only city to have hosted both the [Winter](34069.md) and [Summer Olympics](27566.md).[^cite_note-86]

In terms of international connectedness, as of 2024, Beijing was one of eight cities worldwide that was classified as an "Alpha+" city by the [Globalization and World Cities Research Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network).[^cite_note-87]

## Cityscape



A panorama of the [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City), viewed from the [Jingshan Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jingshan_Park)



Beijing's skyline at dusk, viewed from the [China World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_World_Trade_Center) area

### Architecture

See also: [List of tallest buildings in Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Beijing) and [Chinese architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_architecture)

Three styles of architecture are predominant in urban Beijing. First, there is the traditional architecture of imperial China, perhaps best exemplified by the massive [Tian'anmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tian'anmen) (Gate of Heavenly Peace), which remains the [People's Republic of China's](5405.md) trademark edifice, the [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City), the [Imperial Ancestral Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Ancestral_Temple) and the [Temple of Heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Heaven). Next, there is what is sometimes referred to as the "Sino-Sov" style, with structures tending to be boxy and sometimes poorly constructed, which were built between the 1950s and the 1970s.[^cite_note-88] Finally, there are much more modern architectural forms, most noticeably in the area of the [Beijing CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_CBD) in east Beijing such as the new [CCTV Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCTV_Headquarters), in addition to buildings in other locations around the city such as the [Beijing National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_National_Stadium) and [National Center for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Centre_for_the_Performing_Arts_\(China\)).

  [National Centre for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Centre_for_the_Performing_Arts_\(China\))

In the early 21st century, Beijing has witnessed tremendous growth of new building constructions, exhibiting various modern styles from international designers, most pronounced in the CBD region. A mixture of both 1950s design and [neofuturistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neofuturistic) style of architecture can be seen at the [798 Art Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/798_Art_Zone), which mixes the old with the new. Beijing's tallest building is the 528-meter [China Zun](33141122.md). Since 2007, buildings in Beijing have received the [CTBUH Skyscraper Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CTBUH_Skyscraper_Award) for best overall tall building twice, for the [Linked Hybrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linked_Hybrid) building in 2009 and the [CCTV Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCTV_Headquarters) in 2013. The CTBUH Skyscraper award for best tall overall building is given to only one building around the world every year.

*[Siheyuans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siheyuan)* in Beijing are courtyards surrounding by buildings. Among the more grand examples are the [Prince Gong Mansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Gong_Mansion) and [Residence of Soong Ching-ling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Former_Residence_of_Soong_Ching-ling_\(Beijing\)). These courtyards are usually connected by alleys called *[hutongs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutong)*. The *hutongs* are generally straight and run east to west so that doorways face north and south for good [Feng Shui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feng_Shui). They vary in width; some are so narrow only a few pedestrians can pass through at a time. Once ubiquitous in Beijing, *siheyuans* and *hutongs* are rapidly disappearing,[^cite_note-89] as entire city blocks of *hutongs* are replaced by high-rise buildings.[^cite_note-90] Residents of the *hutongs* are entitled to live in the new buildings in apartments of at least the same size as their former residences. Many complain, however, that the traditional sense of community and street life of the *hutongs* cannot be replaced,[^cite_note-91] and these properties are often government owned.[^cite_note-92]

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Beijing)



[Juyong Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juyong_Pass), viewed from the north. The [Beijing–Zhangjiakou Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Zhangjiakou_Railway) (1909) and [G6 Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Zhangjiakou_Expressway) pass through this historic mountain gateway across the [Great Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China) north of Beijing.

Beijing is situated at the northern edge of the roughly triangular [North China Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_China_Plain), which opens to the south and east of the city. Mountains to the north, northwest and west shield the city and northern China's agricultural heartland from the encroaching desert steppes. The northwestern part of the municipality, especially [Yanqing District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yanqing_District) and [Huairou District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huairou_District), are dominated by the [Jundu Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jundu_Mountains), while the western part is framed by *Xishan* or the [Western Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Hills). The [Great Wall of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China) across the northern part of Beijing Municipality was built on the rugged topography to defend against nomadic incursions from the steppes. [Mount Dongling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Ling_\(Beijing\)), in the Western Hills and on the border with [Hebei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebei), is the municipality's highest point, with an altitude of 2,303 metres (7,556 ft).

Major rivers flowing through the municipality, including the [Chaobai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaobai_River), [Yongding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yongding_River), [Juma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juma_River_\(China\)), are all tributaries in the [Hai River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hai_River) system, and flow in a southeasterly direction. The Miyun Reservoir, on the upper reaches of the Chaobai River, is the largest reservoir within the municipality. Beijing is also the northern terminus of the [Grand Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Canal_of_China) to [Hangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hangzhou), which was built over 1,400 years ago as a transportation route, and the [South–North Water Transfer Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South%E2%80%93North_Water_Transfer_Project), constructed in the past decade to bring water from the [Yangtze River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangtze_River) basin.

The urban area of Beijing, on the plains in the south-central of the municipality with elevation of 40 to 60 metres (130–200 feet), occupies a relatively small but expanding portion of the municipality's area. The city spreads out in concentric [ring roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring_Roads_of_Beijing). The [Second Ring Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Ring_Road_\(Beijing\)) traces the [old city walls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_city_wall) and the [Sixth Ring Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Ring_Road) connects satellite towns in the surrounding suburbs. [Tian'anmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tian'anmen) and [Tian'anmen Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tian'anmen_Square) are at the center of Beijing, directly to the south of the [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City), the former residence of the emperors of China. To the west of [Tian'anmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiananmen) is [Zhongnanhai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongnanhai), the residence of China's current leaders. [Chang'an Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang'an_Avenue), which cuts between Tiananmen and the Square, forms the city's main east–west axis.

Beijing's pattern of development from the old inner city to its urban fringe are frequently described as "spreading like a pancake" (*tan da bing*).[^cite_note-CC-93]<sup>: 135 </sup> This pattern of development is frequently cited as a reason for Beijing's urban problems.[^cite_note-CC-93]<sup>: 135 </sup>

### Climate

Beijing has a [humid continental climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_continental_climate) ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification): *Dwa*), bordering on a monsoonal [cold semi-arid climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_semi-arid_climate) ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification): *BSk*). The city is characterized by hot, humid summers due to the East Asian [monsoon](57630.md) as well as brief but cold, dry winters that reflect the influence of the vast [Siberian anticyclone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siberian_High).[^cite_note-people's_daily-94] Spring can bear witness to sandstorms blowing in from the [Gobi Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gobi_Desert) across the [Mongolian steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongolian-Manchurian_grassland), accompanied by rapidly warming, but generally dry, conditions. Autumn, similar to spring, is a season of transition and minimal precipitation. From 2001 to 2026, the hottest period in Beijing is early August, and the coldest period is early January. According to China's seasonal division standard, Beijing enters spring on 26 March, summer on 20 May, autumn on 13 September, and winter on 31 October. Due to the faster warming in March and September and the slower warming in May and October in recent years, Beijing is generally shorter in autumn than in spring. The average annual temperature (From 1991 to 2020) in the urban area of Beijing is 12.9 °C (55.2 °F) to 13.3 °C (55.9 °F), of which the average daily minimum temperature is 7.7 °C (45.9 °F) to 8.4 °C (47.1 °F), and the average daily maximum temperature is 18.5 °C (65.3 °F) to 18.9 °C (66.0 °F). The average annual temperature in the low-altitude part of the suburbs of Beijing is 11.5 °C (52.7 °F) to 13.0 °C (55.4 °F), of which the average daily minimum temperature is 5.9 °C (42.6 °F) to 8.0 °C (46.4 °F), and the average daily maximum temperature is 18.2 °C (64.8 °F) to 18.7 °C (65.7 °F). The average annual temperature in mountainous areas is between 0 °C (32 °F) and 10 °C (50 °F), decreasing as the altitude increases. The monthly daily average temperature in January is −2.7 °C (27.1 °F), while in July it is 27.2 °C (81.0 °F). [Precipitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precipitation_\(meteorology\)) averages around 528.0 mm (20.79 in) annually ([Haidian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haidian,_Beijing) and [Chaoyang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaoyang,_Beijing) has an average annual precipitation of 584.2 mm (23.00 in)), with close to three-quarters of that total falling from June to August. With monthly percent possible sunshine ranging from 42% in July to 62% in January and February, the city receives 2,490.5 hours of bright sunshine annually. Extremes since 1951 have ranged from −27.4 °C (−17.3 °F) on 22 February 1966 to 41.9 °C (107.4 °F) on 24 July 1999.[^cite_note-95][^cite_note-Mherrera_WB-96]

### Environmental issues

Beijing had a long history of [environmental problems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_problems).[^cite_note-111] Between 2000 and 2009 Beijing's urban extent quadrupled, which not only strongly increased the extent of anthropogenic emissions, but also changed the meteorological situation fundamentally, even if [emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exhaust_gas) of human society are not included. For example, surface [albedo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albedo), wind speed and [humidity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humidity) near the surface were decreased, whereas ground and near-surface [air temperatures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_temperature), vertical air dilution and [ozone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ozone) levels were increased.[^cite_note-112] Because of the combined factors of urbanization and pollution caused by burning of [fossil fuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel), Beijing is often affected by serious environmental problems, which lead to [health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health) issues of many inhabitants. In 2013 heavy [smog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smog) struck Beijing and most parts of northern China, impacting a total of 600 million people. After this "pollution shock" [air pollution](10934212.md) became an important economic and social concern in China. After that the government of Beijing announced measures to reduce air pollution, for example by lowering the share of coal from 24% in 2012 to 10% in 2017, while the national government ordered heavily polluting vehicles to be removed from 2015 to 2017 and increased its efforts to transition the energy system to clean sources.[^cite_note-113]

#### Air quality



A view of the Forbidden City from the Jingshan Park during heavy smog in November 2016

Joint research between American and Chinese researchers in 2006 concluded that much of the city's pollution comes from surrounding cities and provinces. On average 35–60% of the [ozone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ozone) can be traced to sources outside the city. [Shandong](105032.md) Province and [Tianjin](50821.md) Municipality have a "significant influence on Beijing's air quality",[^cite_note-114] partly due to the prevailing south/southeasterly flow during the summer and the mountains to the north and northwest.

In preparation for the [2008 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics) and to fulfill promises to clean up the city's air, nearly US\$17 billion was spent.[^cite_note-sundance-115] Beijing implemented a number of air improvement schemes for the duration of the Games, including halting work at all construction sites, closing many factories in Beijing permanently, temporarily shutting industry in neighboring regions, closing some gas stations,[^cite_note-116] and [cutting motor traffic by half by limiting drivers to odd or even days (based on their license plate numbers)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_space_rationing_in_Beijing),[^cite_note-117] reducing bus and subway fares, opening new subway lines, and banning high-emission vehicles.[^cite_note-octpoll-118][^cite_note-polluteban-119] The city further assembled 3,800 [natural gas](22131.md)-powered buses, one of the largest fleets in the world.[^cite_note-sundance-115] Beijing became the first city in China to require the Chinese equivalent to the Euro 4 [emission standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emission_standard).[^cite_note-120]

Coal burning accounts for about 40% of the [PM 2.5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Particulates) in Beijing and is also the chief source of nitrogen and sulphur dioxide.[^cite_note-MotherJones-121] Since 2012, the city has been converting coal-fired [power stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_major_power_stations_in_Beijing) to burn natural gas[^cite_note-CNDaily20120308-122] and aims to cap annual coal consumption at 20 million tons. In 2011, the city burned 26.3 million tons of coal, 73% of which for heating and power generation and the remainder for industry.[^cite_note-CNDaily20120308-122] Much of the city's air pollutants are emitted by neighboring regions.[^cite_note-MotherJones-121] Coal consumption in neighboring Tianjin is expected to increase from 48 to 63 million tons from 2011 to 2015.[^cite_note-SCMP20130205-123] Hebei Province burned over 300 million tons of coal in 2011, more than all of Germany, of which only 30% were used for power generation and a considerable portion for steel and cement making.[^cite_note-124] Power plants in the coal-mining regions of Shanxi, Inner Mongolia and Shaanxi, where coal consumption has tripled since 2000, and Shandong also contribute to air pollution in Beijing.[^cite_note-MotherJones-121] Shandong, Shanxi, Hebei and Inner Mongolia, respectively rank from first to fourth, among Chinese provinces by coal consumption.[^cite_note-SCMP20130205-123] There were four major coal-fired power plants in the city to provide electricity as well as heating during the winter. The first one (Gaojing Thermal Power Plant) was shut down in 2014.[^cite_note-125] Another two were shut in March 2015. The last one (Huaneng Thermal Power Plant) would be shut in 2016.[^cite_note-ChinaDaily-126] Between 2013 and 2017, the city planned to reduce 13 million tons of coal consumption and cap coal consumption to 15 million tons in 2015.[^cite_note-ChinaDaily-126]

The government sometimes uses [cloud-seeding](449660.md) measures to increase the likelihood of rain showers in the region to clear the air prior to large events, such as prior to the [60th anniversary parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/60th_anniversary_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China) in 2009 as well as to combat drought conditions in the area.[^cite_note-127] More recently, however, the government has increased its usage of such measures as closing factories temporarily and implementing greater restrictions for cars on the road, as in the case of "[APEC blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/APEC_blue)" and "parade blue", short periods during and immediately preceding the [APEC China 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/APEC_China_2014) and the [2015 China Victory Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_China_Victory_Day_Parade), respectively.[^cite_note-128] During and prior to these events, Beijing's air quality improved dramatically, only to fall back to unhealthy levels shortly after.

On 8 and 9 December 2015 Beijing had its first [smog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smog) alert which shut down a majority of the industry and other commercial businesses in the city.[^cite_note-129] Later in the month another smog "red alert" was issued.[^cite_note-abc-2nd-red-130]

According to Beijing's environmental protection bureau's announcement in November 2016, starting from 2017 highly polluting old cars will be banned from being driven whenever Smog "red alerts" are issued in the city or neighboring regions.[^cite_note-131]

In recent years, there has been measurable reductions in pollutants after the "war on pollution" was declared in 2014, with Beijing seeing a 35% reduction in fine particulates in 2017[^cite_note-132] and further reduction by 2020.[^cite_note-CC-93]<sup>: 52 </sup> The primary factors behind this reduction were replacing coal power with natural gas and cleaning up polluting industrial facilities in the Beijing area.[^cite_note-CW-133]<sup>: 169–170 </sup>

Beijing's annual average concentration of major airborne fine particulate matter, or PM2.5, declined to 30 micrograms per cubic meter in 2022, the best air quality for the city since 2013.[^cite_note-134] In 2024, Beijing experienced only two days of heavy pollution, the lowest number on record, which had decreased by 96.6 percent from 58 days in 2013.[^cite_note-135]

##### Readings

Due to Beijing's high level of air pollution, there are various readings by different sources on the subject. Daily pollution readings at 27 monitoring stations around the city are reported on the website of the Beijing Environmental Protection Bureau (BJEPB).[^cite_note-136] [The American Embassy of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Ambassador_to_China) also reports hourly [fine particulate (PM2.5)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Particulates) and [ozone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ozone) levels on Twitter.[^cite_note-Demick-137] Since the BJEPB and US Embassy measure different pollutants according to different criteria, the pollution levels and the impact to human health reported by the BJEPB are often lower than that reported by the US Embassy.[^cite_note-Demick-137]

The smog is causing harm and danger to the population. The air pollution does directly result in significant impact on the morbidity rate of cardiovascular disease and respiratory disease in Beijing.[^cite_note-138] Exposure to large concentrations of polluted air can cause respiratory and cardiovascular problems, emergency room visits, and even death.[^cite_note-139]

#### Dust storms

Dust from the erosion of deserts in northern and northwestern China results in seasonal [dust storms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dust_storm) that plague the city; the [Beijing Weather Modification Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Weather_Modification_Office) sometimes artificially induces rainfall to fight such storms and mitigate their effects.[^cite_note-140] In the first four months of 2006 alone, there were no fewer than eight such storms.[^cite_note-141] In April 2002, one dust storm alone dumped nearly 50,000 tons of dust onto the city before moving on to Japan and Korea.[^cite_note-142]

## Government

Main article: [Politics of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Beijing)

The municipal government is regulated by the [Municipal Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Municipal_Committee_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party) of the [Chinese Communist Party](7175.md) (CCP), led by the [Beijing CCP Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_Secretary_of_Beijing). The Municipal CCP Committee issues administrative orders, collects taxes, manages the economy, and directs a standing committee of the [Municipal People's Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Municipal_People's_Congress) in making policy decisions and overseeing the local government. Since 1987, all CCP Secretary of Beijing is also a member of the [Politburo](51851.md).



Beijing Municipal Civil Affairs Bureau

Government officials include the [mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Beijing) ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language): 市长) and vice-mayor. Numerous bureaus focus on law, public security, and other affairs. Additionally, as the capital of China, Beijing houses all of the important national governmental and political institutions, including the [National People's Congress](38878740.md).[^cite_note-britannica_government-143]

### Administrative divisions

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of administrative divisions of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_administrative_divisions_of_Beijing) and [List of township-level divisions of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_township-level_divisions_of_Beijing).

Beijing Municipality currently comprises 16 administrative [county-level subdivisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County-level_division), namely 16 urban [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_of_China). On 1 July 2010, [Chongwen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chongwen_District) and [Xuanwu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xuanwu_District,_Beijing) were merged into Dongcheng and Xicheng, respectively. On 13 November 2015 [Miyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miyun_District) and [Yanqing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yanqing_District) were upgraded to districts.

#### Towns

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of township-level divisions of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_township-level_divisions_of_Beijing).

Beijing's 16 county-level divisions (districts) are further subdivided into 273 lower third-level administrative units at the [township level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China#Township_level): 119 [towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Town_\(China\)), 24 [townships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Townships_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China), 5 [ethnic townships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_township) and 125 [subdistricts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdistricts_of_China). Towns within Beijing Municipality but outside the urban area include (but are not limited to):

- [Changping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changping_District) 昌平

- [Huairou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huairou) 怀柔

- [Miyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miyun) 密云

- [Liangxiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liangxiang,_Beijing) 良乡

- [Liulimiao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liulimiao) 琉璃庙

- [Tongzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tongzhou_District,_Beijing) 通州

- [Yizhuang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yizhuang,_Beijing) 亦庄

- [Tiantongyuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiantongyuan) 天通苑

- [Beiyuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beiyuan,_Beijing) 北苑

- [Xiaotangshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiaotangshan,_Beijing) 小汤山

Several place names in Beijing end with *mén* (门), meaning "gate", as they were the locations of gates in the former [Beijing city wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_city_fortifications). Other place names end in *cūn* (村), meaning "village", as they were originally villages outside the city wall.

### Judiciary and procuracy

The [judicial system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_system_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China) in Beijing consists of the [Supreme People's Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_People's_Court), the highest court in the country, the Beijing Municipal High People's Court, the [high people's court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_people's_court) of the municipality, three [intermediate people's courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intermediate_people's_courts), one intermediate [railway transport court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railway_Transport_Court_\(People's_Republic_of_China\)?action=edit\&redlink=1), 14 [basic people's court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_people's_court) (one for each of the municipality's districts and counties), one basic railway transport court, and one Internet court. The Beijing No. 1 Intermediate People's Court in Shijingshan oversees the basic courts of Haidian, Shijingshan, Mentougou, Changping and Yanqing.[^cite_note-Xinhuacourts-149] The Beijing No. 2 Intermediate People's Court in Fengtai oversees the basic courts of Dongcheng, Xicheng, Fengtai, Fangshan and Daxing.[^cite_note-Xinhuacourts-149] The Beijing No. 3 Intermediate People's Court in Laiguangying, is the newest of the three intermediate people's courts and opened on 21 August 2013.[^cite_note-Xinhuacourts-149] It oversees the district courts of Chaoyang, Tongzhou, Shunyi, Huairou, Pinggu and Miyun.[^cite_note-Xinhuacourts-149][^cite_note-BJcourts-150] Each court in Beijing has a corresponding [people's procuratorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_People's_Procuratorate).

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Beijing)

As of 2025, Beijing's [GDP](12594.md) was [CN¥](66958.md)5.2 trillion (\$748 billion in nominal, \$1.52 trillion in [PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity)), about 3.71% of the country's GDP and ranked [12th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Chinese_provincial-level_divisions_by_GDP) among [province-level administrative units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_China); its GDP per capita was CN¥238,571 (US\$34,252 in norminal; US\$69,962 in [PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity)) and ranked the [1st](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Chinese_provincial-level_divisions_by_GDP_per_capita) in the country.[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152] It also ranks the tenth largest in the metropolitan economies in the world.[^cite_note-153]

Due to the concentration of [state owned enterprises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_State-owned_enterprises_in_China) in the national capital, Beijing in 2013 had more [Fortune Global 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_Global_500) Company headquarters than any other city in the world.[^cite_note-JLL-154] As of August 2022, Beijing has 54 [Fortune Global 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_Global_500) companies, more than Japan (47), the third-place country after China (145) and the United States (124).[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156] Beijing has also been described as the "[billionaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billionaire) capital of the world".[^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158] Beijing is classified as an Alpha+ (global first-tier) city by the [Globalization and World Cities Research Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network), indicating its influence in the region and worldwide and making it one of the world's Top 10 major cities.[^cite_note-159] In the 2021 [Global Financial Centres Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Financial_Centres_Index), Beijing was ranked as having the sixth-most competitive financial center in the world and fourth-most competitive in the whole Asia & Oceania region (behind Shanghai, Hong Kong and Singapore).[^cite_note-GFCI2-160]

As of 2021, Beijing was ranked first globally in terms of "*Global City Competitiveness*" in the *2020–2021 Global Urban Competitiveness Report* jointly released by the [Chinese Academy of Social Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Social_Sciences) (CASS) and the [United Nations Programme for Human Settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Settlements_Programme) (UN-Habitat).[^cite_note-161]
