### Sector composition



The [Taikoo Li Sanlitun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taikoo_Li_Sanlitun) shopping arcade is a destination for locals and visitors.

The city has a [post-industrial economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-industrial_economy) that is dominated by the [tertiary sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy) (services), which generated 83.8% of output, followed by the [secondary sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_sector_of_the_economy) (manufacturing, construction) at 15.8% and the [primary sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_sector) (agriculture, mining) at 0.26%.[^cite_note-data2022-164] The services sector is broadly diversified with professional services, wholesale and retail, information technology, commercial real estate, scientific research, and residential real estate each contributing at least 6% to the city's economy in 2022.[^cite_note-data2022-164][^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165]



Beijing Financial Street from [Fuchengmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_city_fortifications)

The single largest sub-sector remains industry, whose share of overall output has shrunk to 12.1% in 2022.[^cite_note-data2022-164] The mix of industrial output has changed significantly since 2010 when the city announced that 140 highly-polluting, energy and water resource intensive enterprises would be relocated from the city in five years.[^cite_note-166] The relocation of [Capital Steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shougang_Corporation) to neighboring Hebei province had begun in 2005.[^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168] In 2013, output of automobiles, aerospace products, [semiconductors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semiconductor), pharmaceuticals, and food processing all increased.[^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165]

In the farmland around Beijing, vegetables and fruits have displaced grain as the primary crops under cultivation.[^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165] In 2013, the tonnage of vegetable, edible fungus and fruit harvested was over three times that of grain.[^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165] In 2013, overall acreage under cultivation shrank along with most categories of produce as more land was reforested for environmental reasons.[^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165]

### Economic zones

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of economic and technological development zones in Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_economic_and_technological_development_zones_in_Beijing).



The skyline of [Beijing CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_CBD)



[Zhongguancun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongguancun) is a technology hub in [Haidian District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haidian_District).

In 2006, the city government identified six high-end economic output zones around Beijing as the primary engines for local economic growth. In 2012, the six zones produced 43.3% of the city's GDP, up from 36.5% in 2007.[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170] The six zones are:

1. [Zhongguancun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongguancun), China's silicon village in Haidian District northwest of the city, is home to both established and start-up tech companies. In the first two quarters of 2014, 9,895 companies registered in the six zones, among which 6,150 were based in Zhongguancun.

   [^cite_note-171]

   &#x20;Zhongguancun is also the center of [Beijing-Tianjin-Shijiazhuang Hi-Tech Industrial Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing-Tianjin-Shijiazhuang_Hi-Tech_Industrial_Belt).

2. [Beijing Financial Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Financial_Street), in Xicheng District on the west side of the city between Fuxingmen and Fuchengmen, is lined with headquarters of large state banks and insurance companies. The country's financial regulatory agencies including the [central bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Bank_of_China), [bank regulator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Banking_Regulatory_Commission), [securities regulator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Securities_Regulatory_Commission), and [foreign exchange authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Administration_of_Foreign_Exchange) are located in the neighborhood.

3. [Beijing Central Business District (CBD)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_CBD), is actually located to the east of downtown, near the embassies along the eastern Third Ring Road between Jianguomenwai and Chaoyangmenwai. The CBD is home to most of the [city's skyscraper office buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Beijing). Most of the city's foreign companies and professional service firms are based in the CBD.

4. [Beijing Economic and Technological Development Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Economic_and_Technological_Development_Area), better known as [Yizhuang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yizhuang,_Beijing), is an industrial park the straddles the southern Fifth Ring Road in Daxing District. It has attracted pharmaceutical, information technology, and materials engineering companies.

   [^cite_note-social_economic-172]

5. Beijing Airport Economic Zone was created in 1993 and surrounds the [Beijing Capital International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Capital_International_Airport) in Shunyi District northeast of the city. In addition to logistics, airline services, and trading firms, this zone is also home to Beijing's automobile assembly plants.

6. Beijing Olympic Center Zone surrounds the [Olympic Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Green) due north of downtown and is developing into an entertainment, sports, tourism and business convention center.

[Shijingshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shijingshan), on the western outskirts of the city, was a traditional heavy industrial base of the steel-maker [Shougang Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shougang_Group). After production ceased in 2010, the site was transformed into a center for industrial culture, fairs, art exhibits and sports events, including [a venue for the 2022 Winter Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Air_Shougang).[^cite_note-173] A former chemical industrial park in the far eastern suburbs has been converted into a forest park.[^cite_note-174]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Beijing)

| Year | Pop. | ±% p.a. |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1953 | 2,768,149 | — |
| 1964 | 7,568,495 | +9.57% |
| 1982 | 9,230,687 | +1.11% |
| 1990 | 10,819,407 | +2.00% |
| 2000 | 13,569,194 | +2.29% |
| 2010 | 19,612,368 | +3.75% |
| 2020[^cite_note-BeijingHistoricalGDP-162] | 21,893,095 | +1.11% |
| Population size may be affected by changes on administrative divisions. |  |  |

In 2024, Beijing had a total population of 21.83 million within the municipality, of which 19.26 million (88.2 percent) resided in urban districts or suburban townships and 2.57 million (11.8) lived in rural villages.[^cite_note-data2022-164] The encompassing [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area) was estimated by the [OECD](33853117.md) (Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development) to have, as of 2010, a population of 24.9 million.[^cite_note-175][^cite_note-oecd2015-176]

Within China, the city ranked [second in urban population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_China_by_population) after Shanghai and the [third in municipal population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct-controlled_municipalities_of_China) after Shanghai and Chongqing. Beijing also ranks among the most populous cities in the world, a distinction the city has held for much of the past 800 years, especially during the 15th to early 19th centuries when it was [the largest city in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_cities_throughout_history).

About 13 million of the city's residents in 2013 had local [*hukou* permits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hukou_system), which entitles them to permanent residence in Beijing.[^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165] The remaining 8 million residents had *hukou* permits elsewhere and were not eligible to receive some social benefits provided by the Beijing municipal government.[^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165]

The population increased in 2013 by 455,000 or about 7% from the previous year and continued a decade-long trend of rapid growth.[^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165] The total population in 2004 was 14.213 million.[^cite_note-2004pop-177] The population gains are driven largely by migration. The population's [rate of natural increase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rate_of_natural_increase) in 2013 was a mere 0.441%, based on a [birth rate](515898.md) of 8.93 and a [mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mortality_rate) of 4.52.[^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165] The [gender balance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sex_ratio) was 51.6% males and 48.4% females.[^cite_note-2013_Beijing_Stats-165]

Working age people account for nearly 73.6% of the population.[^cite_note-data2022-164] Compared to 2004, residents age 0–14 as a proportion of the population dropped from 9.95% to 9.92% in 2013, but again increased to 12.1% in 2021.[^cite_note-data2022-164] Residents over the age of 65 declined from 11.12% to 8.58%, but increased to 14.2% in 2021.[^cite_note-data2022-164] From 2002 to 2011, the percentage of city residents with at least some college education nearly doubled, from 20.4% to 37.3%, and further increased to 49.1% by 2021.[^cite_note-data2022-164] About 66.4% have senior secondary school education and 88.2% had reached middle school.[^cite_note-data2022-164]

According to the 2010 census, nearly 96% of Beijing's population are ethnic [Han Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Chinese).[^cite_note-2010census-178] Of the 800,000 ethnic minority population living in the capital, [Manchu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchu_people) (336,000), [Hui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hui_people_in_Beijing) (249,000), [Korean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_people_in_Beijing) (77,000), [Mongol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol) (37,000) and [Tujia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tujia_people) (24,000) constitute the five largest groups.[^cite_note-2010minorities-179] In addition, there were 8,045 Hong Kong residents, 500 [Macau](19068.md) residents, and 7,772 Taiwan residents along with 91,128 registered foreigners living in Beijing.[^cite_note-2010census-178] A study by the Beijing Academy of Sciences estimates that in 2010 there were on average 200,000 foreigners living in Beijing on any given day including students, business travellers and tourists that are not counted as registered residents.[^cite_note-180]

In 2017 the Chinese government implemented population controls for Beijing and Shanghai to fight what it called the "big city disease" which includes congestion, pollution, and shortages of education and health care services. From this policy, Beijing's population declined by 20,000 from 2016 to 2017.[^cite_note-cap-181] Some low-income people are being forcibly removed from the city as both legal and illegal housing is being demolished in some high-density residential neighborhoods.[^cite_note-cap-181] The population is being redistributed to [Jing-Jin-Ji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jing-Jin-Ji) and [Xiong'an New Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiong'an_New_Area), the transfer to the latter expected to include 300,000-500,000 people working in government research, universities, and corporate headquarters.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183]

## Education and research

Main articles: [Education in Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Beijing), [Education in China](407843.md), and [Higher education in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_education_in_China)

See also: [Rankings of universities in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rankings_of_universities_in_China), [List of universities in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_in_China), and [Double First Class University Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_First_Class_University_Plan)



Entrance to the [Imperial University of Peking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_University_of_Peking), c. 1898-1900

Beijing is a world-leading center for scientific and technological innovation. It has been ranked the [No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_scientific_output) city in the world with the largest scientific research output, as tracked by the [Nature Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_Index) since the list's inception in 2016.[^cite_note-SC-37][^cite_note-184][^cite_note-BD-38] When compared to countries, Beijing ranked above Germany, securing third place worldwide after China and the US, according to the Nature Index for 2025. For example, Beijing's share of the 2024 Nature Index is 5,501.45 with 11,915 counts, while Germany's share is 5,000.90 with 10,559 counts.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186] The capital also leads the world with the highest share of articles published in [chemistry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemistry), [physical sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physical_sciences), [Earth & environmental sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earth_and_Environmental_Sciences), and [natural sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_science), especially in the United Nations 17 [Sustainable Development Goals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_Development_Goals) (SDGs)-related output.[^cite_note-187][^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190][^cite_note-191] As of 2024, it stands as the most prolific scientific research center in [biological sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biological_sciences) and [health science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_science) in the Asia-Pacific, ranking third and sixth in the world, respectively.[^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193] According to the [WIPO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Intellectual_Property_Organization) [Innovation Cluster Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innovation_Cluster_Rating), in 2026 the Bejing area ranked 4th among the world's top 100 science and technology [clusters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_cluster).[^cite_note-194]



The West Gate of [Peking University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_University)



Campus of the Peking University

Beijing has over [90 public colleges and universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_and_colleges_in_Beijing), making it the largest urban [public university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_university) system in Asia and the first city in China with the most higher education [institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_and_colleges_in_Beijing).[^cite_note-Beijing's_Universities-195][^cite_note-196] It is also home to the two best universities ([Tsinghua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsinghua_University) and [Peking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_University)) in the whole of the Asia & Oceania region and emerging countries, with their rankings at #11 and #13 places in the world, respectively by the 2027 [Times Higher Education World University Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Times_Higher_Education_World_University_Rankings).[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198][^cite_note-EE-35] Both are members of the [C9 League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C9_League), an alliance of elite Chinese universities offering comprehensive and leading education.[^cite_note-199] Beijing represents more than one-fifth of [147 Double First-Class Universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_First_Class_University_Plan), a national plan to develop elite Chinese universities into world-class institutions by the end of 2050.[^cite_note-200]

According to the [U.S. News & World Report Best Global University Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_&_World_Report_Best_Global_Universities_Ranking) for 2026–2027, Beijing has the highest number of universities among major cities in the world included in the ranking, totaling 31, with 2 universities in the top 20, 7 institutions in the top 200 and 12 institutions in the top 500.[^cite_note-201] According to the [Academic Ranking of World Universities (ARWU)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_Ranking_of_World_Universities) for 2026, Beijing has 2 universities in the top 25, 5 institutions in the top 150, 11 institutions in the top 300, and 16 institutions in the top 500.[^cite_note-202] A number of Beijing's [most prestigious universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rankings_of_universities_in_China) consistently rank among the best in the [Asia-Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific) and the world, including [Peking University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_University), [Tsinghua University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsinghua_University), [Renmin University of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renmin_University_of_China), [Beijing Normal University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Normal_University), [University of Chinese Academy of Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_Chinese_Academy_of_Sciences), [Beihang University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beihang_University), [Beijing Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Institute_of_Technology), [China Agricultural University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Agricultural_University), [Minzu University of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minzu_University_of_China), [University of Science and Technology Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Science_and_Technology_Beijing), [Beijing University of Chemical Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_University_of_Chemical_Technology), [University of International Business and Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_International_Business_and_Economics_\(Beijing\)), [University of Chinese Academy of Social Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chinese_Academy_of_Social_Sciences) and [Central University of Finance and Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_University_of_Finance_and_Economics).[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-BG-31][^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205] These universities were selected as "[985 universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_985)" or "[211 universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_211)" by the Chinese government in order to build world-class universities.[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207]

The city is a seat of the [Chinese Academy of Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Sciences), which has been consistently ranked the No. 1 research institute in the world by *[Nature Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_Index)* since the list's inception in 2014.[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210] The academy also runs the [University of the Chinese Academy of Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_Chinese_Academy_of_Sciences), which is located in Beijing and ranked among the world's top five research institutions by the [Nature Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_Index).[^cite_note-211] Beijing is also a site of the [Chinese Academy of Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Engineering), the [China Academy of Space Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Academy_of_Space_Technology), the [China Academy of Engineering Physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Academy_of_Engineering_Physics), the [Chinese Academy of Social Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Social_Sciences), the [Chinese Academy of Agricultural Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Agricultural_Sciences), the [Chinese Academy of Cultural Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Cultural_Heritage), the [Chinese Academy of Geological Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Geological_Sciences), the [Chinese Academy of Fishery Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Fishery_Sciences), the [Chinese Academy for Environmental Planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_for_Environmental_Planning?action=edit\&redlink=1), the [China Academy of Chinese Medical Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Academy_of_Chinese_Medical_Sciences) and the [National Natural Science Foundation of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Natural_Science_Foundation_of_China).

The city's [compulsory education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_education) system is among the best in the world: in 2018, 15-year-old students from Beijing (together with [Shanghai](27643.md), [Zhejiang](96602.md) and [Jiangsu](166412.md)) outperformed all of the other 78 participating countries in all categories (math, reading, and science) in the [Program for International Student Assessment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Program_for_International_Student_Assessment), a worldwide study of academic performance conducted by the OECD.[^cite_note-PISA2018-212]

## Culture

People native to urban Beijing speak the [Beijing dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_dialect), which belongs to the Mandarin variety of [spoken Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_spoken_language). This speech is the basis for *[putonghua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Putonghua)*, the standard spoken language used in mainland China and [Taiwan](25734.md), and one of the four official languages of [Singapore](27318.md). Rural areas of Beijing Municipality have [their own dialects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jilu_Mandarin) akin to those of Hebei province, which surrounds Beijing Municipality.

Younger residents of Beijing have become more attracted to the nightlife, which has flourished in recent decades, breaking prior cultural traditions that had practically restricted it to the upper class.[^cite_note-213] Today, [Houhai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houhai), [Sanlitun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanlitun) and [Wudaokou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wudaokou) are Beijing's nightlife hotspots.

### Art



Peking opera

Beijing or [Peking opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_opera) is a traditional form of Chinese theater well known throughout the nation. Commonly lauded as one of the highest achievements of [Chinese culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_culture), Beijing opera is performed through a combination of song, spoken dialogue, and codified action sequences involving gestures, movement, fighting and acrobatics. Much of Beijing opera is carried out in an archaic stage dialect quite different from Modern Standard Chinese and from the modern Beijing dialect.[^cite_note-214]

The [cloisonné](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloisonn%C3%A9) (or *Jingtailan*, literally "Blue of [Jingtai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jingtai_Emperor)") metalworking technique and tradition is a Beijing art speciality, and is one of the most revered traditional crafts in China. Cloisonné making requires elaborate and complicated processes which include base-hammering, copper-strip inlay, soldering, enamel-filling, enamel-firing, surface polishing and gilding.[^cite_note-215] Beijing's [lacquerware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lacquerware) is also well known for its sophisticated and intricate patterns and images carved into its surface, and the various decoration techniques of lacquer include "carved lacquer" and "engraved gold".

In 2012 Beijing was named as City of Design and became part of the [UNESCO Creative Cities Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creative_Cities_Network).[^cite_note-216]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Beijing cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_cuisine)



Sliced [Peking duck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_duck) served with traditional condiments

[Beijing cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_cuisine) has been shaped by centuries of imperial court cooking, the culinary traditions of surrounding regions, and the influence of ethnic minorities including Manchu and Mongol peoples. As the imperial capital for over 500 years, Beijing attracted cooks and ingredients from across China, giving rise to a distinctive style that blends northern Chinese staples with court refinement.[^cite_note-217]

[Peking duck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_duck) is perhaps the best known dish. [Fuling jiabing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuling_jiabing), a traditional Beijing snack food, is a pancake (*bing*) resembling a flat disk with a filling made from *[fu ling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fu_Ling)*, a fungus used in traditional [Chinese medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_medicine). [Teahouses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teahouse) are also common in Beijing. [Instant-boiled mutton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instant-boiled_mutton), served in a traditional copper hot pot, is another iconic Beijing dish with origins dating to the Yuan dynasty.

### Places of interest

See also: [Major National Historical and Cultural Sites (Beijing)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_National_Historical_and_Cultural_Sites_\(Beijing\)) and [List of Beijing landmarks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Beijing_landmarks)

According to the *[National Geographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic)*:

> ...the city remains an epicenter of tradition with the treasures of nearly 2,000 years as the imperial capital still on view—in the famed Forbidden City and in the city's lush pavilions and gardens...

— [National Geographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic_Society)[^cite_note-national-218]



[Qianmen Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qianmen_Avenue) is a traditional commercial street outside Qianmen Gate along the southern Central Axis.



[Temple of Heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Heaven) is one of the eight UNESCO World Heritage Sites in Beijing.

At the historical heart of Beijing lies the [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City), the enormous palace compound that was the home of the emperors of the [Ming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty) and [Qing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty) dynasties;[^cite_note-219] the Forbidden City hosts the [Palace Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_Museum), which contains imperial collections of Chinese art. Surrounding the Forbidden City are several former imperial gardens, parks and scenic areas, notably [Beihai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beihai_Park), [Shichahai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shichahai), [Jingshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jingshan_Park) and [Zhongshan Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongshan_Park_\(Beijing\)). These places, particularly Beihai Park, are described as masterpieces of [Chinese gardening](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_garden) art,[^cite_note-220] and are tourist destinations of historical importance;[^cite_note-221] in the modern era, Zhongnanhai has also been the political heart of various Chinese governments and regimes and is now the headquarters of the Chinese Communist Party and the State Council. From Tiananmen Square, right across from the Forbidden City, there are several notable sites, such as the Tian'anmen, [Qianmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qianmen), the [Great Hall of the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Hall_of_the_People), the [National Museum of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_China), the [Monument to the People's Heroes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monument_to_the_People's_Heroes), and the [Mausoleum of Mao Zedong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mausoleum_of_Mao_Zedong). The [Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Palace) and the [Old Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Summer_Palace) both lie at the western part of the city; the former, a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](44940.md),[^cite_note-222] contains a comprehensive collection of imperial gardens and palaces that served as the summer retreats for the Qing imperial family.

Among the best known religious sites in the city is the [Temple of Heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Heaven) (*Tiantan*), located in southeastern Beijing, also a UNESCO World Heritage Site,[^cite_note-223] where emperors of the Ming and Qing dynasties made visits for annual ceremonies of prayers to Heaven for good harvest. In the north of the city is the [Temple of Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Earth) (*Ditan*), while the [Temple of the Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_the_Sun) (*Ritan*) and the [Temple of the Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_the_Moon_\(China\)) (*Yuetan*) lie in the eastern and western urban areas respectively. Other well-known temple sites include the [Dongyue Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Dongyue_Temple), [Tanzhe Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanzhe_Temple), [Miaoying Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miaoying_Temple), [White Cloud Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Cloud_Temple), [Yonghe Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yonghe_Temple), [Fayuan Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fayuan_Temple), [Wanshou Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wanshou_Temple) and [Big Bell Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bell_Temple). The city also has its own [Confucius Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Temple_of_Confucius), and a [Guozijian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guozijian_\(Beijing\)) or Imperial Academy. The [Cathedral of the Immaculate Conception](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_the_Immaculate_Conception_in_Beijing), built in 1605, is the oldest Catholic church in Beijing. The [Niujie Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niujie_Mosque) is the oldest mosque in Beijing, with a history stretching back over a thousand years.



[Universal Studios Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Studios_Beijing)



[Happy Valley Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Happy_Valley_Beijing)

Beijing contains several well-preserved pagodas and stone pagodas, such as the towering [Pagoda of Tianning Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pagoda_of_Tianning_Temple_\(Beijing\)), which was built during the Liao dynasty from 1100 to 1120, and the [Pagoda of Cishou Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pagoda_of_Cishou_Temple), which was built in 1576 during the Ming dynasty. Historically noteworthy stone bridges include the 12th-century [Lugou Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lugou_Bridge), the 17th-century [Baliqiao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baliqiao) bridge, and the 18th-century [Jade Belt Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jade_Belt_Bridge). The [Beijing Ancient Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Ancient_Observatory) displays pre-telescopic spheres dating back to the Ming and Qing dynasties. The [Fragrant Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fragrant_Hills) (*Xiangshan*) is a public park that consists of natural landscaped areas as well as traditional and cultural relics. The [Beijing Botanical Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Botanical_Garden) exhibits over 6,000 species of plants, including a variety of trees, bushes and flowers, and an extensive [peony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peony) garden. The [Taoranting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoranting_Park), [Longtan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longtan_Park), [Chaoyang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Chaoyang_Park), [Haidian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haidian_Park), [Milu Yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milu_Yuan) and [Zizhu Yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purple_Bamboo_Park) parks are some of the notable recreational parks in the city. The [Beijing Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Zoo) is a center of zoological research that also contains rare animals from various continents, including the Chinese [giant panda](12713.md).



[Beijing Railway Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Railway_Museum)

There are 144 museums and galleries (as of June 2008) in the city.[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225][^cite_note-museum-226] In addition to the Palace Museum in the Forbidden City and the National Museum of China, other major museums include the [National Art Museum of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Art_Museum_of_China), the [Capital Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_Museum), the [Beijing Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wanshou_Temple), the [Military Museum of the Chinese People's Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Museum_of_the_Chinese_People's_Revolution), the [Geological Museum of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geological_Museum_of_China), the [Beijing Museum of Natural History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Museum_of_Natural_History) and the [Paleozoological Museum of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleozoological_Museum_of_China).[^cite_note-museum-226]

Located at the outskirts of urban Beijing, but within its municipality are the [Thirteen Tombs of the Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_tombs), the lavish and elaborate burial sites of thirteen Ming emperors, which have been designated as part of the UNESCO World Heritage Site [Imperial Tombs of the Ming and Qing Dynasties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Tombs_of_the_Ming_and_Qing_Dynasties).[^cite_note-227] The archaeological [Peking Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_Man) site at [Zhoukoudian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhoukoudian) is another World Heritage Site within the municipality,[^cite_note-228] containing a wealth of discoveries, among them one of the first specimens of *[Homo erectus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homo_erectus)* and an assemblage of bones of the gigantic [hyena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyena) *[Pachycrocuta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pachycrocuta) brevirostris*. There are several sections of the UNESCO World Heritage Site [Great Wall of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China),[^cite_note-229] most notably [Badaling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badaling), [Jinshanling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jinshanling), [Simatai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simatai) and [Mutianyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutianyu). According to the [World Travel & Tourism Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Travel_and_Tourism_Council) (WTTC), Beijing is the second highest earning tourist city in the world after [Shanghai](27643.md).[^cite_note-230] Theme parks located within the city include [Universal Studios Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Studios_Beijing) and [Happy Valley Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Happy_Valley_Beijing), both of which are among the most visited theme parks in Asia.[^cite_note-231]

### Intangible cultural heritage

The cultural heritage of Beijing is rich and diverse. Starting 2006, the Beijing government started the process of selecting and preserving cultural heritages. Five cultural heritage lists have been published over the years. 288 distinct practices are categorized as cultural heritage. These 288 cultural heritages are further divided into ten categories, namely folk music, folk dance, traditional opera, melodious art, juggling and game, folk art, traditional handicraft, traditional medicine, folk literature and folklore.[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235]

- Folk music&#x20;

  - [Zhihua Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhihua_Temple) music

  - Tongzhou [shanty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_shanty)

- Folk dance&#x20;

  - Tongzhou [Dragon dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragon_dance)

  - Miliangtun [Stilts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stilts)

- Traditional opera&#x20;

  - [Kunqu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunqu)

  - [Peking opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_opera)

- Melodious art&#x20;

  - [Xiangsheng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiangsheng)

- Acrobatic Performance and game&#x20;

  - [Weiqi (Go)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Go_\(game\))

  - [Xiangqi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiangqi)

- Folk art&#x20;

  - [Ivory carving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivory_carving)

- Traditional handicraft&#x20;

  - [Peking duck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_duck) manufacturing techniques

  - [Cloisonné](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloisonn%C3%A9) manufacturing techniques

- Traditional medicine&#x20;

  - [Tong Ren Tang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tong_Ren_Tang) culture

- Folk literature

- Folklore&#x20;

  - [Miaohui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miaohui)

  - [Lantern Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lantern_Festival)

### Religion

The religious heritage of Beijing is rich and diverse as [Chinese folk religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_folk_religion), [Taoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism), [Buddhism](3267529.md), [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism), [Islam](6037917.md) and [Christianity](5211.md) all have significant historical presence in the city. As the national capital, the city also hosts the [State Administration for Religious Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Administration_for_Religious_Affairs) and various state-sponsored institutions of the leading religions.[^cite_note-religion1-236] In recent decades, foreign residents have brought other religions to the city.[^cite_note-religion1-236] According to Wang Zhiyun of the [Chinese Academy of Social Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Academy_of_Social_Sciences), in 2010 there were 2.2 million Buddhists in the city, equal to 11.2% of the total population.[^cite_note-237] According to the Chinese General Social Survey of 2009, Christians constitute 0.78% of the city's population.[^cite_note-Wang2015-238] According to a 2010 survey, Muslims constitute 1.76% of the population of Beijing.[^cite_note-2010-Islam-239]

#### Chinese folk religion and Taoism



[Fire God Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huode_Zhenjun_Temple) near [Di'anmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Di'anmen)

Beijing has many temples dedicated to [folk religious and communal deities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_gods_and_immortals), many of which are being reconstructed or refurbished in the 2000s and 2010s. Yearly sacrifices to the [God of Heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tian) ([祭天](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E7%A5%AD%E5%A4%A9); *jìtiān*) at the [Temple of Heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Heaven) have been resumed by [Confucian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism) groups in the 2010s.

There are temples for the worship of Taoist goddesses ([娘娘](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E5%A8%98%E5%A8%98); *Niángniáng*) in the city, including one primarily dedicated to [Bixia Yuanjun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bixia_Yuanjun) near the [Olympic Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Olympic_Village). [Mount Miaofeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Miaofeng) is a major center of Taoist goddess worship. There are also many temples consecrated to the [Dragon God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragon_King), to the Medicine Master ([药](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E8%8D%AF)[王](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E7%8E%8B); *Yàowáng*), to deified [Guan Yu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guan_Yu#Worship), to the Fire God ([火神](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E7%81%AB%E7%A5%9E); *Huǒshén*), to the [Wealth God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caishen), temples of the [City God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chenghuangshen), and at least one temple consecrated to the [Yellow Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_Emperor) ([轩辕](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E8%BD%A9%E8%BE%95)[黄帝](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E9%BB%84%E5%B8%9D); *Xuānyuán Huángdì*) in [Pinggu District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinggu_District). Many of these temples are governed by the Beijing Taoist Association, such as the [Fire God Temple of the Shicha Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huode_Zhenjun_Temple), while many others are not and are governed by popular committees and locals.&#x20;

The national [Chinese Taoist Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Taoist_Association) and Chinese Taoist College have their headquarters at the [White Cloud Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Cloud_Temple) of [Quanzhen Taoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quanzhen_Taoism), which was founded in 741 and rebuilt numerous times. The [Beijing Dongyue Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Dongyue_Temple) outside Chaoyangmen is the largest temple of [Zhengyi Taoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhengyi_Taoism) in the city. The local Beijing Taoist Association has its headquarters at the [Lüzu Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%BCzu_Temple) near Fuxingmen.[^cite_note-240]

#### Buddhism

| icon | This section **does not [cite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Citing_sources) any [sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability)**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Beijing) by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Referencing_for_beginners). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence). *(October 2017)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |



[Yonghe Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yonghe_Temple) of [Tibetan Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibetan_Buddhism)

11% of the population of Beijing practices [East Asian Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Asian_Buddhism)[^cite_note-241]. The [Buddhist Association of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_Association_of_China), the state's supervisory organ overseeing all [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist) institutions in mainland China, is headquartered in the [Guangji Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangji_Temple_\(Beijing\)), a temple founded over 800 years ago during the [Jin dynasty (1115–1234)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin_dynasty_\(1115%E2%80%931234\)) in what is now [Fuchengmennei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuchengmen) (阜成门内). The Beijing Buddhist Association along with the Buddhist Choir and Orchestra are based in the [Guanghua Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guanghua_Temple_\(Beijing\)), which dates to the Yuan dynasty over 700 years ago. The Buddhist Academy of China and its library are housed in the [Fayuan Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fayuan_Temple) near [Caishikou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caishikou). The Fayuan Temple, which dates to the [Tang dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tang_dynasty) 1300 years ago, is the oldest temple in urban Beijing. The [Tongjiao Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tongjiao_Temple) inside [Dongzhimen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dongzhimen) is the city's only Buddhist nunnery.

The [Xihuang Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xihuang_Temple) originally dates to the [Liao dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liao_dynasty). In 1651, the temple was commissioned by the [Qing Emperor Shunzhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shunzhi_Emperor) to host the visit of the [Fifth Dalai Lama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5th_Dalai_Lama) to Beijing. Since then, this temple has hosted the [13th Dalai Lama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/13th_Dalai_Lama) as well as the [Sixth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobsang_Palden_Yeshe,_6th_Panchen_Lama), [Ninth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thubten_Choekyi_Nyima,_9th_Panchen_Lama) and [Tenth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choekyi_Gyaltsen,_10th_Panchen_Lama) Panchen Lamas.



The White Pagoda of [Miaoying Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miaoying_Temple)

&#x20;The largest Tibetan Buddhist Temple in Beijing is the [Yonghe Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yonghe_Temple), which was decreed by the [Qing Emperor Qianlong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qianlong_Emperor) in 1744 to serve as the residence and research facility for his Buddhist preceptor of [Rölpé Dorjé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changkya_R%C3%B6lp%C3%A9_Dorj%C3%A9) the third [Changkya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changkya_Khutukhtu) (or living Buddha of [Inner Mongolia](55064.md)). The Yonghe Temple is so-named because it was the childhood residence of the [Yongzheng Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yongzheng_Emperor), and retains the [glazed tiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_glazed_roof_tile) reserved for imperial palaces. While the "High-level Tibetan Buddhism College of China", China's highest institution college of Tibetan Buddhism, situated near the Yonghe Temple. The [Lingguang Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingguang_Temple_\(Beijing\)) of [Badachu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badachu) in the [Western Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Hills) also dates to the Tang dynasty. The temple's Zhaoxian Pagoda (招仙塔) was first built in 1071 during the Liao dynasty to hold a tooth relic of the [Buddha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gautama_Buddha). The pagoda was destroyed during the [Boxer Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxer_Rebellion) and the tooth was discovered from its foundation. A new pagoda was built in 1964. The six aforementioned temples: Guangji, Guanghua, Tongjiao, Xihuang, Yonghe and Lingguang have been designated [National Key Buddhist Temples in Han Chinese Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Key_Buddhist_Temples_in_Han_Chinese_Area).



The tomb pagodas at [Tanzhe Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanzhe_Temple)

In addition, other notable temples in Beijing include the [Tanzhe Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanzhe_Temple) (founded in the [Jin dynasty (266–420)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin_dynasty_\(266%E2%80%93420\)) is the oldest in the municipality), the [Tianning Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tianning_Temple_\(Beijing\)) (oldest pagoda in the city), the [Miaoying Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miaoying_Temple) (a [Yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_dynasty)-era white pagoda), the [Wanshou Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wanshou_Temple) (home to the [Beijing Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Art_Museum)) and the [Big Bell Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bell_Temple) (Dazhong Temple).

#### Islam



[Niujie Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niujie_Mosque)

Beijing has about 70 mosques recognized by the [Islamic Association of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Association_of_China), whose headquarters are located next to the [Niujie Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niujie_Mosque), the oldest mosque in the city.[^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243] The Niujie Mosque was founded in 996 during the Liao dynasty and is frequently visited by Muslim dignitaries. The Chinese Muslim community reportedly celebrated [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan) and made [Eid prayers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eid_prayers) at the mosque in 2021.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245]

The largest mosque[^cite_note-246] in Beijing is Changying mosque, located in Chaoyang district, with an area of 8,400 square meters.

Other notable mosques in the old city include the [Dongsi Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dongsi_Mosque), founded in 1346; the Huashi Mosque, founded in 1415; Nan Douya Mosque, near Chaoyangmen; Jinshifang Street Mosque, in Xicheng District; and the Dongzhimen Mosque.[^cite_note-247] There are large mosques in outlying Muslim communities in Haidian, [Madian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madian,_Beijing), Tongzhou, Changping, Changying, Shijingshan and Miyun. The China Islamic Institute is located in the Niujie neighborhood in Xicheng District.

#### Christianity



[Church of the Saviour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_Saviour,_Beijing), also known as the Xishiku Church, built in 1703

##### Catholicism



[St. Joseph's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Joseph's_Church,_Beijing)

In 1289, [John of Montecorvino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_of_Montecorvino) came to Beijing as a [Franciscan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franciscan) missionary with the order from the Pope. After meeting and receiving the support of [Kublai Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kublai_Khan) in 1293, he built the first Catholic church in Beijing in 1305. The [Chinese Patriotic Catholic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Patriotic_Catholic_Association) (CPCA), based in [Houhai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houhai) is the government oversight body for Catholics in mainland China. Notable Catholic churches in Beijing include:

- the Nantang or [Cathedral of the Immaculate Conception](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_the_Immaculate_Conception,_Beijing) also known as the Xuanwumen Church, which was founded in 1605 and whose current archbishop, [Joseph Li Shan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Li_Shan), is one of the few bishops in China to have the support of both the Vatican and the CPCA.

- the Dongtang or [St. Joseph's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Joseph's_Church,_Beijing), better known as the Wangfujing Church, founded in 1653.

- the Beitang or [Church of the Saviour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_Saviour,_Beijing), also known as the Xishiku Church, founded in 1703.

- the Xitang or [Church of Our Lady of Mount Carmel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Our_Lady_of_Mount_Carmel,_Beijing) also known as the Xizhimen Church, founded in 1723.

The [National Seminary of Catholic Church in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Seminary_of_Catholic_Church_in_China) is located in [Daxing District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daxing_District).



[Haidian Christian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haidian_Christian_Church)

##### Protestantism

The earliest Protestant churches in Beijing were founded by [British and American missionaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant_missions_in_China_1807%E2%80%931953) in the second half of the 19th century. Protestant missionaries also opened schools,[^cite_note-248] universities and hospitals which have become important civic institutions. Most of Beijing's Protestant churches were destroyed during the [Boxer Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxer_Rebellion) and afterwards rebuilt. In 1958, the 64 Protestant churches in the city are reorganized into four and overseen by the state through the [Three-Self Patriotic Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three-Self_Patriotic_Movement).

##### Eastern Orthodox

There was a significant amount of [Orthodox Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy) in Beijing. Orthodoxy came to Beijing with [Russian prisoners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albazinians) from the [Sino-Russian border conflicts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-Russian_border_conflicts) of the 17th century.[^cite_note-249] In 1956, Viktor, the bishop of Beijing returned to the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union), and the Soviet embassy took over the old cathedral and demolished it. In 2007, the Russian embassy built a new church in its garden to serve the Russian Orthodox Christians in Beijing.

### Media

#### Television and radio



The [China Central Television Headquarters building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Central_Television_Headquarters_building) in the [Beijing CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_CBD)

[Beijing Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Television) broadcasts on channels 1 through 10, and [China Central Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Central_Television), China's largest television network, maintains its headquarters in Beijing. Three radio stations feature programmes in English: *Hit FM* on FM 88.7, *Easy FM* by [China Radio International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Radio_International) on FM 91.5, and the newly launched *Radio 774* on AM 774. [Beijing Radio Stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Radio_Stations) is the family of radio stations serving the city.

#### Press

The well-known *[Beijing Evening News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Evening_News)*, covering news about Beijing in Chinese, is distributed every afternoon. Other newspapers include *[Beijing Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Daily)*, *[The Beijing News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beijing_News)*, the *[Beijing Star Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Star_Daily)*, the *Beijing Morning News*, and the *[Beijing Youth Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Youth_Daily)*, as well as English-language weeklies *Beijing Weekend* and *[Beijing Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Today)*. The *[People's Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Daily)*, *[Global Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Times)* and the *[China Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Daily)* (English) are published in Beijing as well.



[Beijing Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Daily) headquarters&#x20;

Publications primarily aimed at international visitors and the expatriate community include the English-language periodicals *Time Out Beijing*, *[Beijing This Month](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_This_Month)*, *Beijing Talk*, *[That's Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/That's_Beijing)*, and *[The Beijinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beijinger)*.

#### Beijing rock

Beijing rock (Chinese: 北京摇滚) is a wide variety of rock and roll music made by rock bands and solo artists from Beijing. The first rock band in Beijing was [Peking All-Stars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_All-Stars), which was formed in 1979 by foreigners.

Rock bands and solo artists from Beijing include [Cui Jian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cui_Jian), [Dou Wei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dou_Wei), [He Yong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/He_Yong_\(rock_musician\)), [Pu Shu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pu_Shu), [Tang Dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tang_Dynasty_\(band\)), [Black Panther](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Panther_\(band\)), [The Flowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Flowers_\(Chinese_band\)), [43 Baojia Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Feng_\(singer\)), etc.[^cite_note-250]

## Sports

### Events



A scene from the opening ceremonies of the [2008 Summer Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympic_Games)



[China Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Open_\(tennis\)) in 2009

Beijing has hosted numerous international and national sporting events, the most notables was the [2008 Summer Olympic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics) and [Paralympic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Paralympics) Games and the [2022 Winter Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Winter_Olympics) and the [Paralympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Winter_Paralympics). Other [multisport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multisport_event) international events held in Beijing include the [2001 Summer Universiade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_Summer_Universiade) and the [1990 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Asian_Games). Single-sport international competitions include the [Beijing Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Marathon) (annually since 1981), [China Open of Tennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Open_\(tennis\)) (1993–97, annually since 2004), ISU Grand Prix of Figure Skating [Cup of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cup_of_China) (2003, 2004, 2005, 2008, 2009 and 2010), World Professional Billiards and Snooker Association [China Open for Snooker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Open_\(snooker\)) (annually since 2005), Union Cycliste Internationale [Tour of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_of_Beijing) (since [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Tour_of_Beijing)), 1961 [World Table Tennis Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Table_Tennis_Championships), [1987 IBF Badminton World Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_IBF_World_Championships), the [2004 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_AFC_Asian_Cup) (football), and [2009 Barclays Asia Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Barclays_Asia_Trophy) (football). Beijing hosted the [2015 IAAF World Championships in Athletics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_World_Championships_in_Athletics).

Beijing's [LeSports Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeSports_Center) is one of the main venues for the [2019 FIBA Basketball World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_FIBA_Basketball_World_Cup).[^cite_note-251]



The opening ceremony of the [2022 Beijing Winter Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Beijing_Winter_Olympics)

The city hosted the second [Chinese National Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Games_of_China) in 1914 and the first four [National Games of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Games_of_China) in 1959, 1965, 1975, 1979, respectively, and co-hosted the 1993 National Games with [Sichuan](65185.md) and [Qingdao](250171.md). Beijing also hosted the inaugural [National Peasants' Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Peasants'_Games) in 1988 and the sixth National Minority Games in 1999.

In November 2013, Beijing made a [bid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_bid_for_the_2022_Winter_Olympics) to host the [2022 Winter Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Winter_Olympics).[^cite_note-BZ-23] On 31 July 2015, the International Olympic Committee awarded the 2022 Winter Olympics to the city becoming the first ever to host both Summer and Winter Olympics also for the [2022 Winter Paralympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Winter_Paralympics) becoming the first ever to host both Summer and Winter Paralympics.[^cite_note-IA-24]

### Venues

&#x20;Major sporting venues in the city include the [MasterCard Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MasterCard_Center) at [Wukesong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wukesong_station) west of downtown; the [Workers' Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers'_Stadium) and [Workers' Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers_Indoor_Arena) in [Sanlitun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanlitun) just east of downtown and the [Capital Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_Indoor_Stadium) in Baishiqiao, northeast of downtown. In addition, many universities in the city have their own sport facilities. The [Olympic Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Green) is a stadium cluster centered on the [National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_National_Stadium). It was originally developed for the 2008 Summer Olympics[^cite_note-252] and modified for the 2022 Winter Olympics. The [Big Air Shougang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Air_Shougang) ski jump is in the western suburbs and was built for the 2022 Winter Olympics.[^cite_note-253]

The [Beijing Olympians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Olympians) of the [American Basketball Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Basketball_Association_\(2000%E2%80%93present\)), formerly a [Chinese Basketball Association](939720.md) team, kept their name and maintained a roster of primarily Chinese players after moving to [Maywood, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maywood,_California) in 2005.

[China Bandy Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Bandy_Federation) is based in Beijing, one of several cities in which the potential for [bandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandy) development is explored.[^cite_note-254]

## Transportation

Main article: [Transport in Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Beijing)

Beijing is an important [transport hub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_hub) in [North China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_China) with six [ring roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring_roads_of_Beijing), 1167 km (725 miles) of expressways,[^cite_note-19nj-255] 15 National Highways, nine conventional railways, and six high-speed railways converging on the city.

### Rail and high-speed rail



Tramway at Machiapu Station, 1900



Xizhi Gate station of the Jingzhang Railway, c. 1909

Beijing serves as a large rail hub in [China's railway network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_the_People's_Republic_of_China). Ten conventional rail lines radiate from the city to: [Shanghai (Jinghu Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Shanghai_railway), [Guangzhou (Jingguang Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Guangzhou_railway), [Kowloon (Jingjiu Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Kowloon_railway), [Harbin (Jingha Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Harbin_railway) (including [Qinhuangdao (Jingqin Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Qinhuangdao_railway)), [Baotou (Jingbao Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Baotou_railway), [Chengde (Jingcheng Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Chengde_railway), [Tongliao, Inner Mongolia (Jingtong Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Tongliao_railway), [Yuanping, Shanxi (Jingyuan Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Yuanping_railway) and [Shacheng, Hebei (Fengsha Line)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fengtai%E2%80%93Shacheng_railway). In addition, the [Datong–Qinhuangdao railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Datong%E2%80%93Qinhuangdao_railway) passes through the municipality to the north of the city.

Beijing also has six [high-speed rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-speed_rail) lines: the [Beijing–Tianjin intercity railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Tianjin_intercity_railway), which opened in 2008; the [Beijing–Shanghai high-speed railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Shanghai_high-speed_railway), which opened in 2011; the [Beijing–Guangzhou high-speed railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Guangzhou_high-speed_railway), which opened in 2012; and the [Beijing–Xiong'an intercity railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Xiong'an_intercity_railway) and the [Beijing–Zhangjiakou intercity railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Zhangjiakou_intercity_railway), both of which opened in 2019. The [Beijing–Shenyang high-speed railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Shenyang_high-speed_railway) was completed in 2021.



[Beijing South railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_South_railway_station), one of several in the city

The city's main railway stations are the [Beijing railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_railway_station), which opened in 1959; the [Beijing West railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_West_railway_station), which opened in 1996; and the [Beijing South railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_South_railway_station), which was rebuilt into the city's high-speed railway station in 2008; The [Beijing North railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_North_railway_station), was first built in 1905 and expanded in 2009; The [Qinghe railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qinghe_railway_station), was first built in 1905 and expanded in 2019; The [Beijing Chaoyang railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Chaoyang_railway_station) opened in 2021; The [Beijing Fengtai railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Fengtai_railway_station) opened in 2022; and the [Beijing Tongzhou railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Tongzhou_railway_station) opened in 2025.

Smaller stations in the city including [Beijing East railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_East_railway_station) and [Daxing Airport station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daxing_Airport_station) handle mainly commuter passenger traffic. In outlying suburbs and counties of Beijing, there are over 40 railway stations.[^cite_note-256]

From Beijing, [direct passenger train service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passenger_rail_transport_in_China) is available to most large cities in China. International train service is available to [Mongolia](19271.md), Russia, [Vietnam](202354.md) and [North Korea](21255.md). Passenger trains in China are numbered according to their [direction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railroad_direction) in relation to Beijing.

### Roads and expressways

Official Beijing statistics define civil automobiles as all automobiles registered with the public-security traffic administration and granted civil automobile plates; this category is broader than private automobiles, which are reported separately.[^cite_note-BeijingCivilAutoDefinition-257][^cite_note-BeijingCivilAutos2024-258] The series covers selected years from 1949 to 1965 and annually from 1966 through 2024; the historical display is attributed to the National Bureau of Statistics, and the 2024 endpoint is 647.6 in units of 10,000 vehicles.[^cite_note-QianzhanBeijingCivilAutos-259][^cite_note-BeijingCivilAutos2024-258] The preliminary 2025 value is excluded.[^cite_note-BeijingCivilAutos2025Preliminary-260]

Year-end civil automobile ownership in Beijing municipality, in 10,000 vehicles, for selected years 1949–1965 and annually from 1966 through 2024. The measure covers public-security-registered automobiles bearing civil plates and is broader than private automobiles.[^cite_note-BeijingCivilAutoDefinition-257][^cite_note-BeijingCivilAutos2024-258] Historical coverage through 2012 is NBS-attributed.[^cite_note-QianzhanBeijingCivilAutos-259] Official Beijing releases extend the series through finalized 2024; the preliminary 2025 value is outside the bounded coverage.[^cite_note-BeijingCivilAutos2025Preliminary-260]

[**Raw data**](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Data\:Beijing%20civil%20automobile%20ownership.tab)

Further information: [Expressways of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressways_of_Beijing) and [China National Highways of Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_National_Highways_of_Beijing)



View of [4th Ring Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_Ring_Road) in [Chaoyang District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaoyang_District,_Beijing)

Beijing is connected by road links to all parts of China as part of the National Trunk Road Network. Many [expressways of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressways_of_China) serve Beijing, as do 15 [China National Highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_National_Highway). Beijing's urban transport is dependent upon the [ring roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring_Roads_of_Beijing) that concentrically surround the city, with the [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City) area marked as the geographical center for the ring roads. The ring roads appear more rectangular than ring-shaped. There is no official "1st Ring Road". The [2nd Ring Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2nd_Ring_Road) is located in the inner city. Ring roads tend to resemble [expressways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled-access_highway) progressively as they extend outwards, with the [5th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5th_Ring_Road) and [6th Ring Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6th_Ring_Road) being full-standard national expressways, linked to other roads only by interchanges. Expressways to other regions of China are generally accessible from the [3rd Ring Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3rd_Ring_Road_\(Beijing\)) outward. A final outer orbital, the [Capital Area Loop Expressway (G95)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G95_Capital_Area_Loop_Expressway), was fully opened in 2018 and will extend into neighboring [Tianjin](50821.md) and [Hebei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebei).



[Beijing–Tongzhou Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Tongzhou_Expressway)

Within the urban core, city streets generally follow the checkerboard pattern of the ancient capital. Many of Beijing's boulevards and streets with "inner" and "outer" are still named in relation to gates in the city wall, though most gates no longer stand. Traffic jams are a major concern. Even outside of rush hour, several roads still remain clogged with traffic.

Beijing's urban design layout further exacerbates transportation problems.[^cite_note-261] The authorities have introduced several bus lanes, which only public buses can use during rush hour. In the beginning of 2010, Beijing had 4 million registered automobiles.[^cite_note-262] By the end of 2010, the government forecast 5 million. In 2010, new car registrations in Beijing averaged 15,500 per week.[^cite_note-263]

Towards the end of 2010, the city government announced a series of drastic measures to tackle traffic jams, including limiting the number of new license plates issued to passenger cars to 20,000 a month and barring cars with non-Beijing plates from entering areas within the Fifth Ring Road during rush hour.[^cite_note-264] More restrictive measures are also reserved during major events or heavily polluted weather.

To obtain a valid license plate, Beijing drivers must be selected in a lottery.[^cite_note-CW-133]<sup>: 168 </sup> As part of government policy support for the use of electric vehicles, Beijing drivers with fully electric cars have a much greater chance of being selected for a license plate.[^cite_note-CW-133]<sup>: 168 </sup> Additionally, fully electric vehicles are exempt from restrictions on which day of the week a driver may drive their vehicle.[^cite_note-CW-133]<sup>: 168 </sup>

Road signs began to be standardized with both Chinese and English names displayed, with location names using pinyin, in 2008.[^cite_note-265]

### Air

#### Beijing Capital International Airport



Beijing Capital International Airport in 1959



Terminal 3 of the [Beijing Capital International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Capital_International_Airport)

Beijing has two of the world's largest airports. The [Beijing Capital International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Capital_International_Airport) ([IATA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IATA): PEK), located 32 kilometres (20 mi) northeast of central Beijing in [Chaoyang District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaoyang_District,_Beijing) bordering [Shunyi District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shunyi_District), is the [second busiest airport in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World's_busiest_airports_by_passenger_traffic) after [Atlanta](3138.md)'s [Hartsfield–Jackson International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hartsfield%E2%80%93Jackson_Atlanta_International_Airport).[^cite_note-ACI_aero-19] Capital Airport's Terminal 3, built during the expansion for the 2008 Olympics, is one of the largest in the world. Capital Airport is the main hub for [Air China](297958.md) and [Hainan Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hainan_Airlines). The [Airport Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airport_Expressway_\(Beijing\)) and [Second Airport Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2nd_Airport_Expressway) connects the airport to the northeast and east of the city center, respectively. Driving time from city center is about 40 minutes under normal traffic conditions. The [Capital Airport Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_Airport_Express) line of [Beijing Subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Subway) and the [Capital Airport Bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Airport_Bus) serves the Beijing Capital International Airport.

#### Beijing Daxing International Airport



Beijing Daxing International Airport

The [Beijing Daxing International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Daxing_International_Airport) (IATA: PKX) located 46 kilometres (29 mi) south of the city in [Daxing District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daxing_District) bordering the city of [Langfang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Langfang), [Hebei Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebei_Province), opened on 25 September 2019.[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-carnoc20160112-267][^cite_note-268] The Daxing Airport has one of the world's largest terminal buildings and is expected to be a major airport serving Beijing, Tianjin and northern Hebei Province. Daxing Airport is connected to the city via the [Beijing–Xiong'an intercity railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing%E2%80%93Xiong'an_intercity_railway), the [Daxing Airport Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daxing_Airport_Express) line of the Beijing Subway and two expressways.

#### Other airports

With the opening of the Daxing Airport in September 2019, the [Beijing Nanyuan Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Nanyuan_Airport) (IATA: NAY), located 13 kilometres (8.1 mi) south of center in [Fengtai District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fengtai_District), has been closed to civilian airline service. Other airports in the city at Liangxiang, Xijiao, Shahe and Badaling are primarily for military use.

#### Visa requirements for air passengers

As of June 2026, visitors from 53 countries are permitted a 240-hour visa-free transit visit in Beijing, where the 240 hours is calculated from midnight of the day after entry and visitors are only permitted to stay within Beijing.[^cite_note-269] Nationals from 50 countries are also permitted a 30-day visa-free stay in China.[^cite_note-270]

### Public transit



Map of Beijing Subway



An articulated [Beijing bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_bus)

The [Beijing Subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Subway), which began operating in 1969, now has 25 lines, 459 [stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Beijing_Subway_stations), and 783 km (487 mi) of [lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_length). It is the [longest subway system in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_metro_systems) and [first in annual ridership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro_systems_by_annual_passenger_rides) with 3.66 billion rides delivered in 2016. In 2013, with a flat fare of [¥](66958.md)2.00 (US\$0.31) per ride with unlimited transfers on all lines except the [Airport Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airport_Express,_Beijing_Subway), the subway was also the most affordable [rapid transit system in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit_in_China). The subway is undergoing rapid expansion and is expected to reach 30 lines, 450 stations, 1,050 kilometres (650 mi) in length by 2022. When fully implemented, 95% of residents inside the [Fourth Ring Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_Ring_Road) will be able to walk to a station in 15 minutes.[^cite_note-271] The [Beijing Suburban Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Suburban_Railway) provides commuter rail service to outlying suburbs of the municipality.

As part of the urban re-development for the 2008 Olympics, Beijing's subway system was significantly expanded.[^cite_note-CC-93]<sup>: 137 </sup> On 28 December 2014, it switched to a distance-based fare system from a fixed fare for all lines except the [Airport Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airport_Express,_Beijing_Subway).[^cite_note-english.cri.cn-272] Under the new system a trip under 6 km (3+1⁄2 mi) will cost [¥](66958.md)3.00(US\$0.49), an additional [¥](66958.md)1.00 will be added for the next 6 km (3+1⁄2 mi) and the next 10 km (6 mi) until the distance for the trip reaches 32 km (20 mi).[^cite_note-english.cri.cn-272] For every 20 kilometres (12 miles) after the original 32 kilometres (20 miles) an additional [¥](66958.md)1.00 is added.[^cite_note-english.cri.cn-272] For example, a 50-kilometre (31-mile) trip would cost [¥](66958.md) 8.00.

There are nearly 1,000 [public bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Bus) and [trolleybus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybus) lines in the city, including four [bus rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_rapid_transit) lines. Standard bus fares are as low as ¥1.00 when purchased with the *[Yikatong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yikatong)* metrocard.

### Taxi



Beijing EU5 Taxi in Beijing

Metered [taxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxicab) in Beijing start at ¥13 for the first 3 kilometres (1.9 mi), ¥2.3 [Renminbi](66958.md) per additional 1 kilometre (0.62 mi) and ¥1 per ride fuel surcharge, not counting idling fees which are ¥2.3 (¥4.6 during rush hours of 7–9 am and 5–7 pm) per 5 minutes of standing or running at speeds lower than 12 kilometres per hour (7.5 mph). Most taxis are [Hyundai Elantras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyundai_Elantra), [Hyundai Sonatas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyundai_Sonata), [Peugeots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peugeot), [Citroëns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citro%C3%ABn) and [Volkswagen Jettas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen_Jetta). After 15 kilometres (9.3 mi), the base fare increases by 50% (but is only applied to the portion *over* that distance). Different companies have special colours combinations painted on their vehicles. Usually registered taxis have yellowish brown as basic hue, with another color of Prussian blue, hunter green, white, umber, tyrian purple, rufous, or sea green. Between 11 pm and 5 am, there is also a 20% fee increase. Rides over 15 km (9 mi) and between 23\:00 and 06\:00 incur both charges, for a total increase of 80%. Tolls during trip should be covered by customers and the costs of trips beyond Beijing city limits should be negotiated with the driver. The cost of unregistered taxis is also subject to negotiation with the driver.

### Bicycles



Huilongguan–Shangdi bicycle lane

Beijing has long been well known for the number of bicycles on its streets. Although the rise of motor traffic has created a great deal of congestion and bicycle use has declined, bicycles are still an important form of local transportation. Many cyclists can be seen on most roads in the city, and most of the main roads have dedicated [bicycle lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicycle_lanes). Beijing is relatively flat, which makes cycling convenient. The rise of [electric bicycles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bicycles) and [electric scooters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_scooters), which have similar speeds and use the same cycle lanes, may have brought about a revival in bicycle-speed two-wheeled transport. It is possible to cycle to most parts of the city. Because of the growing traffic congestion, the authorities have indicated more than once that they wish to encourage cycling, but it is not clear whether there is sufficient will to translate that into action on a significant scale.[^cite_note-273] On 30 March 2019, a 6.5 km (4 mile) bicycle-dedicated lane was opened, easing the traffic congestion between [Huilongguan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huilongguan) and [Shangdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shangdi) where there are many high-tech companies.[^cite_note-274] Cycling has seen a resurgence in popularity spurred by the emergence of a large number of dockless app based [bikeshares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicycle-sharing_system) such as [Mobike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobike), [Bluegogo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bluegogo) and [Ofo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ofo_\(bike_sharing\)) since 2016.[^cite_note-275]

## Defence and aerospace
